みずほ経済 EXPRESS

日本の原油輸入価格は今後さらに上昇へ 
～物価上振れリスクが強まれば、一段の利上げ加速も～

調査部 主任エコノミスト 東深澤 武史 
池田 亮平 
エコノミスト 阿部 大樹 
シニア日本経済エコノミスト 越山 祐資 
080-1069-4919  takeshi.higashifukasawa@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 日銀は物価上振れ抑制に舵。中東情勢に起因する物価押し上げリスクは予断を許さず 
9 月日銀会合後の会見で植田総裁は「政策局面が変化した」と述べた（図表 1） 。これまで日銀は、
基調的物価の押し上げを金融政策運営の重点としてきたが、足元では 2％前後の物価上昇が定着しつ
つあるとみられる。こうしたもとで、金融政策運営の重点は物価上振れの回避に移っており、そのた
めに緩和的な金融環境をなるべく早く脱することが重視される。日銀の基本的なスタンスが、連続利
上げや、0.25％の利上げ幅を上回る大幅利上げは回避するのが望ましいというものであることも踏ま
えると、今後の利上げペースは 3 カ月に一回程度、0.25％ずつが基本になるだろう 1。 
ただし、金融政策の重点が物価上振れ回避にシフトしているなかでは、よりフォワードルッキング
な政策運営が求められる 2。したがって、 原油調達コストの上昇や円安など、 物価上振れリスクの強ま
り次第で利上げペースが速まる可能性がある。先行きの物価を押し上げる主なリスク要因として、日
図表1 9月会合後の総裁会見のポイント 図表2 WTI原油先物

### Page 2

2

銀は、原油高等の中東情勢影響、AI 関連需要の強さ、円安の三点を挙げている。このうち、原油価格
は足元で再び上昇しており、先行きの不確実性も大きい。中東情勢は、物価を取り巻く現下最大のリ
スクだろう。 
■ 不安定な情勢下で再び原油価格が上昇。タンカー運賃も急騰しダブルパンチの様相 
WTI原油先物は、9月に入って一時1バレル=100ドル台と、5月中旬以来の水準に上昇した （図表2） 。
足元では原油価格は幾分低下しているものの、水準としては依然高い状態が続いている。背景には、
中東産原油の供給懸念が強まったことがある。7月下旬以降、 サウジアラビアとイエメン・フーシ派に
よる戦闘が開始し、 ホルムズ海峡を代替する重要な原油輸送ルートとして活用されてきたバブ ・ エル ・
マンデブ海峡の通航リスクが高まった。 また、9月に入り、 サウジアラビアの原油パイプラインが攻撃
を受けて停止したと伝わると、中東産原油の域外輸送が一段と困難になるとの見方が広がった。 
中東情勢が再度悪化するなかで上昇しているのは原油価格だけではない。原油価格の上昇を大幅に
上回る勢いで、原油タンカーの運賃が急騰している（図表3）。8月以降に上昇基調が鮮明となり、9月
中旬には一時、中東情勢悪化前（2026年初）の約46倍、情勢悪化後の3～7月頃と比べても約3.2倍に達
した。 海峡航行リスクが高まるもと、 積み替えや迂回によって原油輸送の効率が悪化しているためだ。
海事データを活用して商品市場を分析しているベルギーの調査会社Kplerによると、 ホルムズ海峡の内
側にあるペルシャ湾から原油を運び出す船と、海峡の外側から最終目的地まで運ぶ船を分け、洋上で
原油を積み替える方式が広がっているようだ。 長距離輸送を担う船が危険な航路を通らずに済む反面、
積み替え作業や待機に時間を要するため、船を次の輸送に回しにくくなる。また、危険な海域を避け
るために遠回りする輸送も増え、船が一度の航海に費やす日数も長くなっている。より多くの輸送資
源が必要となったことで船舶需給がひっ迫し、タンカー運賃が大きく押し上げられている格好だ。

図表3 原油タンカーの運賃 図表4 原油輸入単価と原油価格

■ 今後は日本の原油調達コストが上昇。企業間取引価格は反転加速を予想 
原油価格の上昇に原油タンカー運賃の急騰が重なり、先行きの日本の原油輸入単価（単位量あたり
の原油調達コスト）は一段と上昇する見込みだ3。 中東産原油が日本に輸送されるまでには1～2カ月程
度の期間を要するため、8月中旬以降の原油価格上昇 ・原油タンカー運賃上昇は10月以降の原油輸入単
価を押し上げ、その後国内の企業間取引価格へと影響が波及していくと考えられる。足元の原油価格
とタンカー運賃を踏まえると、10月の原油輸入単価は1バレル＝約140ドル程度と、中東情勢悪化後で
最も高い水準に上昇する可能性が高い（図表4）。この影響を輸入全体の平均価格（円建てベース）で
みると、10月の伸びは前年比＋29％程度と、中東情勢悪化後に最も輸入物価の伸びが加速した6・7月
に迫る勢いになる計算だ。さらに、その後は、石油化学製品などの関連財を中心に、国内の企業間取
引価格が上昇しよう。 足元で上昇圧力が一服しつつある国内企業物価は再び加速し、11月頃に同＋7％
台の伸びになると予想する（図表5）。 
加えて、原油輸入金額が膨らんで貿易収支が悪化すれば、円安圧力が強まる可能性がある。原油の
代替調達が進展した6月以降は1兆円に迫る貿易赤字が続いているが、特に10月以降は原油輸入単価が
上昇するもとで本邦貿易赤字が拡大する公算が大きく、実需面からの円安圧力が強まりやすい（図表
6） 。 足元の為替相場は、 米国の利上げ観測を背景に円安に振れやすい地合いだ。 そこに実需の動きが
重なり、1ドル＝160円を上回るようなドル高円安が進行すれば、輸入物価の上振れを通じて、国内企
業物価の伸びが一層高まるリスクがある。 
■ 物価上昇圧力が長期化する場合は利上げペース加速も視野に 
以上のように、当面の企業間取引価格に対しては押し上げ圧力が持続する公算が大きい。物価上昇
圧力が長期化するほど消費者物価への価格転嫁姿勢も強まる可能性があるだけに、日銀は物価上振れ
に対する高い警戒レベルを維持することになろう。 
仮に物価上振れリスクが大きく高まる場合には、 連続利上げ ・大幅利上げの可能性も排除できない。
中東情勢の混乱が長期化し、27年以降も原油輸入単価が一段と上振れることが見込まれる場合、ある

### Page 4

4

いは1ドル=160円を持続的に上回る水準まで円安が進展する場合には 、連続利上げや大幅利上げのシ
ナリオが現実味を帯びてくるだろう。

1 中立金利の水準は不透明であり、利上げの影響はタイムラグをともなって現れる。連続利上げ・大幅利上げによって意図せず
金融引き締めに転じれば、経済へのマイナス影響が必要以上に強まるリスクがある。 
2 中東情勢悪化以降で最初の利上げを決定した 6 月会合を振り返っても、日銀は川上の企業物価の加速を理由の一つとして利上
げに踏み切っている。6 月会合の声明文において日銀は、国内企業物価上昇率が前年比＋6.3％（5 月時点の速報ベース）に加速
したことを踏まえ、 「企業間取引におけるやや速いスピードでの価格転嫁が、消費者段階における幅広い品目の価格上昇に波及
していく可能性がある」と評価した。 
3 中東情勢悪化以降、日本の原油輸入単価は国際市況から大きく上振れている。背景として、主な代替調達先である米国産原油
が需給ひっ迫の懸念等から値上がりし、割高になったことなどが挙げられる。経済産業省発表によると、秋口以降は中東産原油
の調達が増加し、割高な米国産原油に対する依存度は低下すると見込まれるものの、本文記載のように中東産原油の輸送コスト
が大幅に高まっていることから、先行きも割高感の解消は見込みづらい。

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 13 日
上週重點訊息
◼ TOPIX 啟動大規模改革：日本交易所集團 10 月 7 日公布 TOPIX 調整結果，符合新制納入標
準的成分股將由 9 月底的 1,636 檔降至 986 檔，另有 35 檔股票新納入。現有成分股中，共
有 683 檔未符合流動性及浮動市值等標準，這些股票將自 2026 年 10 月底起逐步降低權
重，並於 2027 年 10 月重新評估，屆時仍未達標者將於 2028 年 7 月底降至零。此次改革有
助於改善 TOPIX 成分股的流動性及投資價值，並促使企業更加重視市場評價。

◼ 巴西總統選舉波索納洛首輪領先魯拉：巴西最高選舉法院 10 月 5 日公布首輪投票結果，參
議員弗拉維奧・波索納洛取得 47.03%的有效票，現任總統魯拉獲得 45.16%，兩人均未取得
過半票數，將於 10 月 25 日進行第二輪決選。若最終由波索納洛當選將相較有利於巴西股市
表現。
◼ IEA 支持加速釋出既有儲油：國際能源署（IEA）10 月 7 日表示，會員國支持加快釋出 3 月
已承諾提供的儲油，並將優先增加柴油供應。截至目前，會員國已釋出約 3.25 億桶儲油；若
剩餘承諾全數執行，可再向市場提供約 1 億桶供應，有助緩解運輸業與農業面臨的燃料成本
壓力。隨各國持續釋出戰略儲備石油，將有利於在短期內壓抑全球油價進一步上行。
市場展望
◼ 標普 500 獲利預估連續上修，資訊科技為主要推升來源：標普 500 企業獲利預估連續兩季上
修，其中資訊科技產業為主要改善來源，反映 AI、雲端運算及資料中心需求仍然強勁。雖然
美國資料中心仍面臨電力與基礎設施瓶頸，但建置規模持續擴大，AI 投資也逐步延伸至記憶

體、網路設備、資安及企業應用等領域。因此，預期科技類股的獲利動能仍具支撐，具 AI 供
應鏈優勢的台股與日股亦可望延續相關成長動能。
◼ 中天期債券可望兼顧息收與利率風險：美國經濟仍具韌性，加上政府與企業發債供給維持高
檔，可能使市場利率維持在較高水準。雖然投資級債信用利差位於相對低檔，但企業獲利、
現金流及償債能力仍具支撐，較高的美國公債基準利率也使其利息收益維持一定水準。短天
期債券面臨利率下降後的再投資風險，長天期債券則較易受到公債供給及利率波動影響，因
此中天期美國公債及投資級債可望在息收與利率風險之間維持較為平衡的表現。

2【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
26Q4結論-上游材料加速更新，漲價仍為主旋律
1.PCB上游:供不應求推動漲價動力，新材料應用下帶來新機會
• 玻纖布:E-Glass(高階)/LowDK-2/T-Glass 在2026Q4~2027年呈現全面緊缺，成為推動當前上游材料漲價的主要
因素，也是決定結構性缺貨的關鍵原料，玻布廠迎來產品組合改善+報價上揚雙重催化。
• 銅箔:HVLP4加速驗證日台以外的供應商，中系擴產積極+中系設備加速採用下，維持2027年供需缺口放緩預期。
• 銅箔基板:PTFE樹脂為美系客戶解決互聯的確定性解方，成為銅箔基板材料中新晉路線；傳統的PPO/CH樹脂仍
有一片天，26Q4 VR300 設計將定案，PTFE+M8/M9設計呼聲較高。
• 鑽針:高階鍍膜針需求攀升，供應商在2026~2028年加快投入建置產能，新材料(PTFE)仍有一定的鑽針消耗，產
品組合的改變遠比漲價來的重要。
2. IC載板: 產能全數售罄，缺料與缺產能並行，關注供應商漲價態度。
• 供需缺口:維持2026/2027年 20%+/40%+ 缺口預期。

• 主邏輯為面積放大+層數增加，大廠產能售罄，客戶需要付出更高的價格預定產能+料源。
3. PCB:關注AWS、Google新品放量，以及2027~2028年HDI設計的變化，聚焦Tier1供應體系和伺服器
級HDI稀缺產能。
4. 建議首選關注個股:
• 高階稀缺HDI產能:2368金像電、3037欣興、4958臻鼎
• 產品組合優化顯著:1303南亞、1815富喬、8021尖點、8039台虹
• 缺貨27~28年不緩解+AI Agent受惠:8046南電、3189景碩、3037欣興、4958 臻鼎

5【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
表1:OBBA法案和AI行動計畫
全年核心:政策主導AI為基本方針
法案 大而美法案(One Big Beautiful Bill Act) AI行動計劃(America's AI Action Plan)
內容 1. 下調聯邦所得稅:從35%下調至21%，
減輕企業稅賦負擔。
2. 恢復2017年減稅與就業法案項目中
允許企業當年資本支出可以全額抵
減稅賦(折舊扣除)，有利重資本支出
廠商加快投資回收時間，受惠廠商
如CSP大廠。
3. 國內R&D全額費用化: 為了獎勵企業
在美國進行生產研發，符合條件的
R&D費用可以於當年度全面費用化，
受惠廠商如Open AI、Anthropic等。
1. 加速AI創新:確保美國擁有全球最強
大的AI系統，放鬆相關管制與障礙，
並鼓勵開源等。
2. 增加AI基礎設施建置:提升美國AI算
力基礎設施硬體基礎，以滿足AI推
動經濟和安全的需求；加快關鍵項
目的審核，加快建設資料中心、晶
圓代工廠、電力基礎設施，掃除一

切AI基礎設施擴充的障礙。
3. 維護國際外交與AI安全:在全球AI競
爭中掌握規則制定權，輸出美國AI
技術，同時阻止對手利用美國創新
超越美國，嚴防高階算力流向競爭
國家，向盟友出口美國AI算力技術
等，有利主權AI(Sovereign AI)的發
展。。
資料來源:福邦投顧整理
• AI 成為美國政策上的基本方針，OBBBA法案刺激企業加大投資AI算力，AAAP計畫掃除建
置AI算力的瓶頸和主權AI方針的確立。

• PCB設計日益複雜，耗用大量產能：過去伺服器板層數約16~2X層，目前主流已經來到
20~30層，未來30~50層越顯常見。
• PCB用量增加，耗用大量產能:隨著各家設計陸續參考NVIDIA Rack設計，除了原有的主
板(GPU/ASIC 使用)，亦新增更多副板，例如Switchboard、midplane等，終端客戶積極
驗證新供應廠商。

### Page 12

12【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
PCB鑽針需求呈現翻倍成長-CCL材料影響
◼ PCB用鑽針受到板材影響，在同樣板厚下，每針可鑽孔次數大幅縮水:
• M6~M7時主針鑽孔壽命約600~800次
• M8材料下可鑽400~500次
• M9材料下僅剩下100~200次
• 材料越高階，鑽針使用量會是過往的6倍以上。
過去 目前 未來
對應材質 M6~M7 M8/(有布/無布+球型矽粉) M9~M10
主針壽命 1000/600~800次 400~500次 100~200次
表5:鑽針基於不同板材的使用壽命&各應用對應的鑽針方案

### Page 13

13【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
PCB鑽針需求呈現翻倍成長-長徑比帶動報價上揚
• 當前主流GPU用PCB約5mm厚度，隨著AI用PCB板厚的提升，每增加1mm，製造上可能會採
用增加一根預鑽針，採用預鑽針預鑽至特定深度，後續用主針完成加工。
• GPU伺服器:GB200採用「1主針配2白針」→GB300採用「1主針配3白針」→VR200採用「
1主針配4白針」，
• AI用的鑽針，長度/直徑比超過30規格的鑽針，報價上會採用一個方案一個價格，因為
加工精度要求提高，難度變大；目前AI用的鍍膜鑽針報價較傳統白針多20~50%，若是
這種報價方式，特別是板厚超過7mm，產品售價將會多40~50%；未來約在2027~2028年
時，7.5~8.5mm的鑽針將成為主流，報價將會是一般白針的6倍以上報價。
• 毛利率方向:AI用的鍍膜鑽針鑽針，根據產業訪查，產品毛利率約50%+。
GPU Server ASIC Server 交換機

+1~4根預鑽白針
2主針

擠效應，白針類產品外溢給凱崴等廠商。

### Page 20

20【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
• 下個世代的半導體封裝需要更大的面積來放置更多元件、支持2D~3D封裝(CoWoS~SOIC)
，因此材料的要求需要Low CTE、高彈性變量來達到低翹曲度（Warpage)和連接可靠度
。
• 根據Resonac白皮書，為了解決內埋元件載板對於核心層的厚度精度要求，較薄的核心
層或不均勻的厚度，會導致內埋元件的損壞，因此之後許多載板核心的設計會採用厚
Core+ low CTE 膠片（採用T/Q Glass)。
• 2026年的TPU，2027年的Maia300都有見到這類核心Core CCL+PP的設計方案
(Multilayer Core)，此也會多消耗T-Glass的供應量。
T-Glass:客戶積極導入抗翹曲設計
圖5:Resonac Type F產品

### Page 30

30【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
無布VS.有布方向選擇
⚫ PTFE 由於Dk/Df的性能優於電子布，如果PTFE搭配電子布，會導致其Dk/Df轉差。
⚫ 未來走向:
1. 有布方案(M9~M10搭配LowDK2~4電子布):若PCB上須放置CPU/GPU/ASIC等晶片，考量尺寸
安定性因素，會需要搭配具有電子布的銅箔基板，例如UBB、ComputeBoard、
SwitchBoard、MotherBoard。
2. 無布方案(搭配無布膠片):若僅考連傳輸性能(不放置晶片)，目前採取的無布方案，樹
脂填矽粉/球型矽微粉的方案為主流，以改善膨脹性。

主要以球型粉料為主。

### Page 32

32【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
PCB製造端的製程變化-銅漿燒結
⚫ 銅漿燒結製程源於瞬態液相燒結(TLPS)技術，主要用於實現PCB盲/埋孔電路層間的Z軸
互連。
⚫ 過去傳統高多層多採用背鑽的製程，但在NV陣營，開始嘗試使用”銅漿燒結“的製程來
處理高多層版的訊號連接，以解決背鑽精度控制的問題。
⚫ 目前具有【燒結製程+大板製造能力】的主要為板廠，例如深南電路、景旺電子、勝宏
科技、滬電股份、生益電子；台系則有金像電具備此製程能力。
圖10:PCB銅燒結概念示意圖
資料來源:產業調研、福邦投顧整理

• 目前Nvidia的供應來看，主要會以生益科技為第一供應商(合作最久)，第二供應商則從
非中國供應商中選擇，目前與生益科技與台虹入列的優先供應商，生益科技具有
CCL+PP(PTFE/CH)的製造能力，台虹PTFE CCL (PFA)可搭配生益、台光電等CCL的CH PP
方案，亦能搭配自產的PFA PP，可提供客戶彈性的產能需求。
• 其餘具有PTFE樹脂CCL開發能力的供應商，開發進度較落後，不在考量範圍內。
• PCB段的生產: 以目前客戶的意願上，採用無布方案為優先目標，PCB生產良率約在
10~30%，主要量產生產廠商預期為深南電路、景旺電子、勝宏科技、滬電股份；欣興僅
參與配合RD階段(台郡負責燒結部分,但設備有限制)。

38【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
OpenClaw:加速從LLM往AI Agent 發展
◼ OpenClaw:開放式AI助理項目，可以根據使用者指令在應用程式和線上服務之間自行處
理複雜任務，並被設計為可以代替使用者執行任務的AI軟體，並非過往的聊天機器人。
◼ OpenAI推出GPT5.4，此次更新提供Thinking和Pro兩種模型； Pro主要適用於需要進行
複雜任務的使用者，Thinking模型可以在產出答案前提出思考計畫，使用者可以從中調
整任務方向→強化Agent能力，可以支持智慧裝置執行複雜的工作流程。
◼ NVIDIA將推出類似OpenClaw的開放式平台-NemoClaw。
資料來源:產業調研、福邦投顧整理

### Page 36

39【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
Agentic AI的瓶頸主要來自CPU
◼ 根據論文《A CPU-Centric Perspective on Agentic AI》一文指出，可以看出執行時間中
CPU延遲占了大比例（紅圈），最高可達到90%以上；吞吐量瓶頸除了來自GPU，亦會
來自CPU;能耗方面，大批次處理中(Batch size提升至128)，CPU平行處理效率低於GPU
，佔到總能耗超過44%，延遲會從2.8秒提升至6秒以上，超過2倍。
資料來源: 《A CPU-Centric Perspective on Agentic AI》 、福邦投顧整理
說明:Haystack RAG（問答檢索）/Toolformer（數學運算與 API 呼叫）/ChemCrow（化學文獻檢索）
Langchain（網路搜尋與摘要）/SWE-Agent（程式碼與 Bash/Python 執行)
圖11:Agent下，CPU佔據了大部分的延遲

### Page 37

40【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
CPU朝向多核發展→推升載板面積
◼ Agent執行:API處理、資料解析、腳本執行，90%由CPU完成，為了降低延遲，CPU往多
核心發展是趨勢。
◼ 記憶體與上下文切換:CPU需要頻繁在不同Agent狀態做切換，多核心架構能讓每個
Agent有獨立的處理單元，降低上下文切換帶來的效率損耗。
◼ 沙盒隔離/安全:Agent 需要大量獨立的一般伺服器建立Sandbox來執行，使用量與使用
者和使用量正相關，需要更多核心數、高頻寬。
資料來源:Intel、AMD、福邦投顧整理預估

傳統光通訊模組板-HDI/mSAP供應體系的新機會
◼因應高度整合和小型化需求，光模組開始大量採用MSAP來縮小產品體積和損耗；進入
1.6T，L/S 要求微縮至20µm-25µm；因此1.6T開始，強制採用MSAP。
◼材料規格上，亦往M7~M8等級以上升級，但在MSAP部分，會大量採用載體銅箔(三井金屬
90%市佔率)、日東紡的Low CTE布，目前有面臨缺料的情況。

交換機板:主流AI層數較低，傳統乙太網多層為主
◼ 資料中心需要承載流量大+高速傳輸，在此需求下，高多層板成為此需求的解決方案，
其優點在於電子元件間連線縮短下，訊號傳輸速度提高，而且也方遍佈線。
◼乙太網交換機為各家CSP+主權主要Scale out的主流，800G報價是400G的2倍以上，1.6T
是800G的4倍以上。

料價格則會較400G材料增加60%+，Tomahawk6 將會使用於2026年的1.6T交換機，
可使用材料約M8U~M9-(碳氫+LK2)等級，2027年後的規格可望往M9發展。

Document 058: 2026年9月进出口数据解读：全球AI投资热潮带动外需大幅走强，9月出口超预期高增

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月进出口数据解读：全球AI投资热潮带动外需大幅走强，9月出口超预期高增.pdf
Report source: 东方金诚

1
全球 AI 投资热潮带动外需大幅走强，9 月出口超预期高增
——2026 年 9 月进出口数据解读
研究发展部执行总监 冯琳
根据 10 月 14 日海关总署公布的数据，以美元计价，2026 年 9 月出口额同比增长 36.3%，8 月为增
长 25.0%，1-9 月出口额累计同比增长 21.3%，2025 年全年为 5.4%；9 月进口额同比增长 31.0%，8 月为
增长 28.2%，1-9 月进口额同比增长27.5%，2025 年全年为0.3%。
一、9 月出口增速高位上行，主要原因是全球AI 投资热潮对芯片等直接相关商品出口的拉动效应增
大，以及当月外需全面走强，各类商品出口增速都有显著加快。
以人民币计价，9 月出口、进口额同比增速分别为29.6%和 24.6%，与同期以美元计价的增速差主要
源于过去一年中人民币对美元汇价升值。
以美元计价，9 月出口额同比增长 36.3%，增速较上月大幅加快 11.3 个百分点，显著超出市场预期。
在上年同期基数稳定的背景下，9 月出口增速大幅加快，除夏季极端天气影响全面消退外，背后主要有

三个推动因素：一是全球 AI 投资热潮持续发酵，带动芯片等相关商品出口增速高位上行。数据显示，9
月集成电路（芯片）出口额同比增长136.0%，增速较上月加快 5.4 个百分点；当月自动数据处理设备及
其零部件出口额同比增长 92.9%，增速加快 15.9 个百分点。9 月以上两类 AI 直接相关商品出口增速加
快，影响整体出口增速上行1.8 个百分点。
二是 AI 投资热潮对全球经济的推动作用大于中东冲突带来的影响，9 月外需进一步走强，带动我国
各类商品出口增速全面加快。数据显示，9 月纺织纱线织物及其制品、箱包、服装、玩具、家电等五大
类劳动密集型商品合计出口额同比增长25.2%，增速较上月加快16.1 个百分点。背后是9 月摩根大通全
球制造业PMI 达到53.0%，高于8 月的52.3%，升至年初以来最高水平，显示当前全球经济增长动能持续
改善，外需随之走强。另外，9 月韩国、越南出口增速分别为 83.5%和 39.4%，较 8 月加快 14.8 和 13.1
个百分点，都与当月我国出口增速高位上行相印证。

三是近年国内制造业转型升级取得进展，对新能源汽车、高技术产品出口的 推动作用持续显现。数
据显示，9 月我国汽车出口量同比增长 55.5%，出口金额同比增长 53.2%，增速分别较上月加快 18.2 和
18.8 个百分点，其中，当月新能源乘用车出口量同比增长163.5%，增速加快8.8 个百分点。这既受我国
汽车在全球市场竞争力增强推动，也与国内车市竞争激烈、汽车销量下行等因素有关。9 月高新技术产

### Page 2

2
品出口额同比增长63.6%，增速较上月加快6.5 个百分点，在整体出口额中的占比达到32.9%，较去年同
期上升 5.5 个百分点，显示我国出口商品结构持续改善。
从出口目的地来看，9 月我国对主要贸易伙伴出口增速多现大幅加快。其中，当月对美、欧、日等
发达经济体出口分别同比增长39.6%、25.3%、15.8%，增速分别加快 5.2、18.6、7.6 个百分点，需要指
出 的是，9 月对欧盟出口增速显著加快，也不排除一定程度上存在 10 月中欧经贸谈判前的抢出口效应
影响；9 月对共建一带一路国家和地区出口同比增长 35.0%，增速较上月加快 11 个百分点。整体上看，
9 月各类主要商品出口增速、对主要贸易伙伴出口增速呈现全面加快态势。
展望未来，考虑到外需偏强态势会有一定惯性，预计10 月出口增速有可能保持 30%左右的高位，年
底前出口高增势头有望延续。不过需要指出的是，7 月以来全球 AI 估值大幅调整，美国费城半导体指数
月均同比涨幅由 6 月的 157.3%降至 9 月的97.1%，10 月初以来在进一步下行。历史规律显示，该指标通

常领先我国芯片出口增速 3 至 4 个月。这样来看，后期芯片及自动数据处理设备及其零部件出口增速有
高位下行趋势，或带动整体出口增速回落。总体上看，在外需对宏观经济拉动力增强的背景下，四季度
进一步出台总量型稳增长政策的迫切性下降。
2、9 月进口额同比高位加速，主要受三方面因素影响：一是极端天气对沿海港口外贸运输的干扰全面消
退；二是我国对外贸易“大进大出”模式下，出口高增会直接拉动相关进口需求 ；三是国际油价上涨推动原
油进口额同比增速回升，对当月进口总额增长的拉动作用由负转正。
以美元计价，9 月进口额同比增长31.0%，增速较上月加快2.8 个百分点，继续处于高增区间。9 月
进口增速加快，主要有两方面原因：一是台风等极端天气对沿海港口外贸运输的干扰全面消退，这也是
当月出口增速明显加快的一个原因；二是我国对外贸易有“大出大进”特征，加工贸易占比较高，出口
高增必然会带动进口增速处于高位；三是 9 月中东局势升级推高国际油价，当月原油进口额同比增长
3.3%，增速较上月回升12.1 个百分点，对进口总额同比增速由上月的下拉1.0 个百分点转为拉动0.3 个

百分点。
从其他主要进口商品来看， 9 月集成电路进口均价延续上涨，同比涨幅小幅放缓至 71.7%（前值
72.1%），加之进口量同比受基数走高影响放缓至 1.7%（前值 6.7%），当月进口额同比增速较上月回落
8.9 个百分点，但仍处74.7%的高增水平；9 月铁矿石进口价格小幅回落，同比下跌1.9%（前值3.0%），
进口量因基数走高同比下降 5.6%（前值为增长 3.2%），共同拖累进口额同比下降 7.4%（前值为增长
6.2%）；9 月未锻轧铜及铜材进口量增加，同比降幅收窄至-5.9%（前值-11.6%），加之供需偏紧背景下
进口均价同比涨幅加快至 46.5%（前值 45.4%），当月进口额同比增速加快至 37.9%（前值 28.5%）；9

### Page 3

3 
月大豆进口均价同比上涨 11.4%（前值 8.6%），但因进口量同比降幅扩大至-7.7%（前值-1.1%），当月
进口额同比增速较上月放缓4.5 个百分点至2.8%。 
展望后续，由于出口仍将延续较快增长，集成电路进口将维持高增，加之稳增长政策发力会在一定
程度上提振内需，预计10 月进口额同比将保持30%左右的高增水平。

Document 068: 261005_daiwa_GUC

Source PDF: all report pdf/261005_daiwa_GUC.pdf

### Page 1

Summary: Global Unichip (GUC) reported September revenue which closed its 3Q26 top-
line 19% above market expectations thanks to its stronger-than-expected turnkey revenue 
run-rate during the quarter, likely attributable to more capacity support from its key foundry 
helping GUC meet strong demand from customers for AI projects. We see upside risk to 
our/consensus 3Q26 bottom-line estimates but are currently comfortable with our 4Q26 
forecasts despite the 3Q26 revenue beat likely dwarfing QoQ growth for 4Q26. We have a 
Buy (1) rating on the counter and will review our model in due course post its quarterly 
results release.

Highlight 
• 3Q26 top-line beating expectations. GUC released September revenue of TWD7.1bn, 
up 22% MoM (+98% YoY, see chart below), which closed its 3Q26 top-line at 
TWD18.8bn, up 35% QoQ (+118% YoY) for a new record. 3Q26 revenue beat our and 
consensus estimates both by 19% each thanks to its turnkey business (ie, mass-
production) which contributed TWD16.7bn for 89% of total and beat our estimate by 
22%. We attribute the beat to likely its key foundry continuously unleashing extra 
capacity helping GUC meet the strong customer AI demand during the quarter.

• Quarterly results preview and outlook. We expect GUC to have earned 3Q26 net 
profit of TWD1.7bn, varying not much from the consensus estimate, yet given the top-
line beat, we now see upside risk to the market expectations. As for 4Q26, our top-line 
and bottom-line forecasts are currently 10% and 26% above the consensus, 
respectively, making us comfortable with our current forecasts since we see the 3Q26 
beat being driven by increased foundry capacity allocation for some shipment pull-in, 
thus dwarfing 4Q26’s QoQ growth on intact AI demand. As flagged in our previous note 
(Upgrading: unleashed capacity adding to growth, 5 August 2026), we continue to 
expect GUC to be a key beneficiary from the strong AI ASIC ramps, including AI CPU 
and accelerators.

Recommendation 
We rate GUC Buy (1) with a 12M TP of TWD 5,125 on our 4-quarter forward PER target of 
63x. The stock performed well recently which we see as largely pricing in the 3Q26 top-line 
beat, therefore only suggesting investors accumulate only on any technical pullback. Key 
downside risks are less foundry support and margin execution.

Document 069: 261005_daiwa_TW-CCL

Source PDF: all report pdf/261005_daiwa_TW-CCL.pdf
Report source: line報告備份

### Page 1

Summary: After market hours on 5 October 2026, EMC, TUC, and ITEQ released their 
monthly sales results. Our read-through is positive as the three key CCL vendors beat 
sales expectations by 5-15% on better pricing and on-going spec upgrades. Our thesis is 
that the strong AI demand is causing a spill-over impact on the whole CCL industry with 
low-end CCL having c.100% price hike in 2026E and high-end CCL seeing a 40-50% price 
hike due to cost inflation in 2026E. Recent data points showed this thesis remains valid. 
Looking ahead, we expect high-end/low-end CCL to see additional c.10%/20% ASP 
increases in 4Q26E, which could be the key share price catalyst. We believe that recent 
negatives news (including the impact of PTFE and CPO/NPO on the industry) are 
overdone. The impact on high-end CCL from these new technologies is likely to be limited 
from the point of view of Taiwan CCL companies. As such, we remain positive that the 
three names in our CCL coverage will ride on the decent price hike trend given favourable 
supply-demand dynamics.

• EMC (2383 TT, TWD5,700, Buy [1]) 
EMC’s September 2026 revenue was TWD21.5bn (+7% MoM, +169% YoY), which led to 
its 3Q26 sales (TWD60.9bn; +29% QoQ, +142% YoY) reaching 112% of our 3Q26 forecast 
(TWD54.4bn) and 110% of the Bloomberg consensus forecast (TWD55.5bn). We attribute 
the revenue strength to the higher-than-expected price hike and on-going spec upgrades 
(leading to higher ASPs). Management attributes c.50% of the MoM increase in revenue in 
September to the price hike and the other 50% came to product mix optimisation. Looking 
ahead, the company doesn’t rule out the potential to increase its ASP again in 4Q26 (after 
the first time in 2Q26) in order to reflect rising raw material prices since 2Q26. As such, we 
expect revenue to grow at least high teens again on the potential price head as well as 
ongoing spec upgrades.

EMC sees limited impact from PTFE and HC glass-fibre-free CCL. Based on our 
understanding, PTFE is still under certification accounting for only limited layers inside 
specified high-end PCB as of now. As such, its impact on the high-end CCL TAM should be 
limited. As for HC glass-fibre-free CCL, the rigidity of PCB using this type of CCL could be 
a key concern to us.

Lastly, regarding the recent Co-Packaged Optics/Near-Packaged Optics (CPO/NPO) 
news’s negative impact on the CCL TAM, we also believe it to be limited given the likely 
adoption date for CPO of 2030, based on EMC’s forecast. Besides, we believe the PCB 
board inside CPO scale-up switches at that time would still need to support an 800G/1.6T 
transmission speed (or even lower) which would still require high-end PCB transmission 
although the highest transmission bandwidth can increase to 3.2T via optics. Lastly, EMC 
believes the spec of CCL won’t downgrade to M4 in the worst-case scenario but would be 
at least M6. Last but not least, we believe this kind of CPO-connected AI server might only

EPS forecast. Key downside risk: weaker-than-expected AI server demand.

• TUC (6274 TT, TWD1,780, Buy [1]) 
TUC’s September 2026 revenue was TWD6.1bn (+4% MoM, +115% YoY), which led to its 
3Q26 sales (TWD17.9bn; +26% QoQ, +123% YoY) reaching 103% of our 3Q26 forecast 
(TWD17.4bn) and 105% of the Bloomberg consensus forecast (TWD17.1bn). Looking 
ahead, we expect TUC to deliver at least QoQ growth in 4Q26 given 1) a price hike along 
with its peers for high-end CCL (eg. M8) and 2) some shipment delay from 3Q26 given 
flooding in Southeast Asia. For the longer term, the company is promising a continual spec 
upgrade trend in 2027 with new projects using M9-grade CCL (including low dk1, low dk2, 
and Q-glass version) in 2027 for high-end switches, AI servers, and optical transceivers. 
Considering the insufficient capacity in the CCL industry, we expect TUC to further optimise 
its product mix after acquiring more high-end CCL orders in future quarters (vs. a single 
major AI server client in 2026). We have a Buy (1) rating on TUC, with a 12-month TP of 
TWD2,320, based on a target PER of 35x applied to our 1-year forward EPS forecast. Key 
downside risks: 1) weaker-than-expected demand for switches/AI servers, and 2) market 
share loss to CCL peers.

• ITEQ (6213 TT, TWD751, Buy [1]) 
ITEQ’s September 2026 revenue was TWD5.8bn (+15% MoM, +111% YoY), which led to 
its 3Q26 sales (TWD15.6bn; +35% QoQ, +102% YoY) reaching 114% of our 3Q26 forecast 
(TWD13.7bn) and 108% of the Bloomberg consensus forecast (TWD14.5bn). The number 
for 3Q26 was higher than our estimate due likely to a stronger-than-expected price hike 
(c.30% in 3Q26 vs. expectation of 20%). Looking ahead to 4Q26, we expect the company 
to further raise its pricing by 20-30% to factor in cost inflation in raw materials (E-glass) 
and the supply-demand gap. The company also expects the rising supply of e-glass (which 
could lift its utilisation rate from the current level of 75%) to lift its gross margin more 
significantly. For 2027, the company is excited about the new CPU adoption driving rising 
M7 CCL usage and is working on M9 certification with new end customers, which could 
become a key growth driver in 2028. We have a Buy (1) rating on ITEQ. Key downside 
risk: weaker-than-expected regular server demand.

In the interests of timeliness, this document has not been edited.

### Page 1

EQUITY: HANDSETS
Largan Precision   3008.TW 3008 TT
Global Markets Research
5 October 2026
Highly sensitive to CPO FAU growth
CPO on track, likely to contribute c.18% of 2028F 
earnings; but near-term Apple demand could be lighter
Action: Maintain Buy; TP raised to TWD8,300, implying ~23% upside
We reiterate Buy on Largan, and raise our TP to TWD8,300, based on 30x 2028F EPS of 
TWD276.7 (previously: TWD6,000, on 25x 2028F EPS of TWD240.0). We cut our earnings 
forecasts by 7.2% and 10.4% for 2026F and 2027F, respectively, to reflect the adverse 
impact of rising BOM costs on smartphone demand and potential cannibalization of the 18 
Pro series by the iPhone Duo. For the first time we incorporate the co-packaged optics fiber 
array unit (CPO FAU) revenue into our earnings model, by assuming the new business 
would contribute 1%/18% of 2027/28F earnings. Hence, our 2028F earnings imply 44% 
y-y growth, driven by CPO FAU and Apple’s (AAPL US, Not rated) camera upgrades. We 
raise our target P/E from 25x to 30x to reflect Largan’s smooth progress in CPO FAU and a 
higher sensitivity to its earnings. Every one million units of CPO FAU could contribute 
c.TWD10 EPS to Largan, in our calculation. The target P/E of 30x exceeds the high end of 
the stock’s historical trading band (10-25x) since 2015. Largan trades at 35x 2027F P/E.
CPO FA/FAU progress on track
As we highlighted in June  and July , Largan can offer a full-range of CPO FAU components 
and modules, including prism/microlens array (PMLA), FA, and FAU, and has the strength to 
fully automate the FA and FAU assembly process by leveraging its strong internal machine 
tool capability. By doing so, Largan’s time-to-market and cost structures for FA and FAU 
assembly should be competitive vs peers. We estimate the total CPO FAU market could 
grow from less than 5mn units in 2027F to 30-40mn units in 2028F, and we expect 
Largan’s market share to be at 10%/15% in 2027/28F (Fig. 1 ). We assume the ASP to be 
over USD100, as its major customer will likely prefer Largan to handle FA/FAU assembly 
rather than just supply components. We believe its fully-automated pilot run line was 
ready by end-3Q26, and the first MP line could be ready by 1H27F. 
Apple: Sunny’s market share loss and continued camera upgrades in 2027-28F
Sunny (2382 HK, Neutral) earlier faced some yield issues with the new variable aperture (VA) 
main camera lens for the iPhone 18 Pro series, and this could benefit Largan and Genius 
(3406 TT, Buy). However, given the headwinds of rising memory costs and the potential 
cannibalization of the 18 Pro series by the iPhone Duo, we see slower iPhone lens shipments 
from end-2026F to 1H27F, before the 20th anniversary iPhone (Pro Max) and the second-gen 
iPhone Duo are introduced in 2H27F. We expect a further upgrade in iPhone Pro’s main 
camera in 2H27F, and introduction of a new periscope camera for the Pro Max in 2H28F.

Company profile
Largan is a technology leader in handset camera lenses, and is currently Top 2 plastic lens maker in the world.
Valuation Methodology
Our TP of TWD8,300 is based on 30x 2028F EPS of TWD276.7. Our target P/E of 30x exceeds the high end of the stock’s historical trading band
(10-25x) since 2015. The benchmark index for the stock is TAIEX.
Risks that may impede the achievement of the target price

than-expected ASP/margin erosion; 4) slower-than-expected spec upgrades; 5) increased competition, causing market share loss; and 6) worse-
than-expected production yields.
ESG
Largan focuses on resource recycling and voluntarily reported green house gas emission through attending ISO14064 in 2020. Largan has achieved
90%+ water recycling and targets to save 1% electricity every year.

Definition of Nomura Group's equity research rating system and sectors
The rating system is a relative system, indicating expected performance against a specific benchmark identified for each individual stock, subject to 
limited management discretion. An analyst’s target price is an assessment of the current intrinsic fair value of the stock based on an appropriate 
valuation methodology determined by the analyst. Valuation methodologies include, but are not limited to, discounted cash flow analysis, expected 
return on equity and multiple analysis. Analysts may also indicate expected absolute upside/downside relative to the stated target price, defined as

SECTORS
A 'Bullish' stance, indicates that the analyst expects the sector to outperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Neutral' stance, 
indicates that the analyst expects the sector to perform in line with the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Bearish' stance, indicates that the 
analyst expects the sector to underperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. Sectors that are labelled as 'Not rated' or shown as 'N/A' are 
not assigned ratings. Benchmarks are as follows: United States: S&P 500; Europe: Dow Jones STOXX 600; Global Emerging Markets (ex-Asia): 
MSCI Emerging Markets ex-Asia. Japan/Asia ex-Japan: Sector ratings are not assigned.

Target Price
A Target Price, if discussed, indicates the analyst’s forecast for the share price with a 12-month time horizon, reflecting in part the analyst's 
estimates for the company's earnings. The achievement of any target price may be impeded by general market and macroeconomic trends, and by 
other risks related to the company or the market, and may not occur if the company's earnings differ from estimates.

LG에너지솔루션 (373220)

Company Brief 
이차전지/석유화학 
Analyst 노우호 
wooho.rho@meritz.co.kr 
RA 황현식 
hyeonsig.hwang@meritz.co.kr Long Trading 구간  
3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후: ESS ↑, BEV ↓  
LG에너지솔루션의 3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후 배터리 섹터는 11/3 미국 중간선거
를 전후로 Trading Long 투자의견이 유효하다. 미국 중간선거 이후 특정정당 우세와 
별개로, 미국 Ex-China ESS 공급망 프리미엄은 유효하다. 동사 역시 그간의 ESS 
사업 난제(단위당 수율, 팩 병목)는 점진적 해소될 점이다. 반면 시장의 BEV 반등 기
대감에 동의하지 않는다. EU 프랑스와 독일의 내연기관 퇴출시점 지연 움직임 및 미
국 연비규제 정책 폐지 결정으로 전통 완성차 고객사들향 BEV 배터리 출하 부진은 
여전하겠다. 미국의 BEV 정책 변화시점은 28.11월 대선으로 지연될 전망이다.

ESS 모멘텀: 한국 제3차 ESS 중앙계약시장 사업자 선정 모멘텀 
대다수 시장 참여자들은 2026년 연말까지 LG에너지솔루션의 미국 ESS 60Gwh  규
모의 잔여 수주 달성 가시성에 집중한다. 해당 ESS 사업 수주 가이던스 관건은 시기
보다 방향성이다. 단, 당사가 주목하는 연말 모멘텀은 한국 정부향 제3차 ESS 중앙
계약시장 사업자 선정으로 판단한다. 3차 규모는 1.13Gw로 1&2차 계약 규모(합산 
1.13Gw) 대비 확대되었다. 또한 언론보도상 LG에너지솔루션은 해당 수주를 위한 핵
심 4대 소재 등 전 Value-chain 국산화를 선언한 바 있다. 해당 수주 확정시, 동사
를 비롯한 해당 Value-chain에 긍정적 모멘텀이 예상된다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 51만원 제시   
26년 연중 지정학 Risk, 에너지 대전환 영향 등에 전력 인프라 수요는 꾸준할 전망
이다. 향후 국내 이차전지 산업은 ‘L’ Shaped Cycle 전망, 반면 동사는 스마트카 T사
향 원통형 전지 출하량 증가, ESS 제조업 이상으로 변모하는 과정으로 산업 평균 성
장률 Outperform 가능하다. 투자의견 Buy 유지, 적정주가는 추정치 상향에 51만원
을 제시한다.

Meritz Research  2026. 10. 15

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 510,000원 
현재주가 (10.14) 422,000원

Document 202: Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

By Advanced Economies Team
Advanced Economies:
Resilience to shock after shock
October 2026

### Page 2

• The global economy is in the midst of a growth cycle. As a result, GDP growth is more resilient to shocks than initially
anticipated.
• The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise in oil prices, whose negative impact on growth is offset
by a series of reflationary forces.
• The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial intelligence (AI).
• The world’s major economies are currently engaged in a broader push to expand their capital goods investment.

• While European and US households are experiencing more or less the same energy price shock, inflation and household
consumption remain higher in the US.
• Higher and above-target inflation calls for monetary tightening.
• Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks examined in this publication (the US, the UK, the
Eurozone). Inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026 or Q1 2027, but there is a significant risk that energy inflation will

spread further to other components of inflation in the future.
• Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength (in the US).
• Monetary policies also have to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal policy (particularly in Japan).
• The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: the ensuing rise in energy prices contributes to the recent increase in
long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the start of the year) to levels close to those of 2002.

• The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period of growth), reflecting different fiscal trajectories. In the
US, the UK, France and Germany the public debt-to-GDP ratio is increasing, while it has stabilized in Italy and decreased in
Spain and Japan.
• The rise in long-term interest rates will weigh on public finances. The fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it will be in 2030.
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with GDP growth.

For more information, please refer to our publication:
EcoPerspectives – Advanced Economies | 3rd quarter of 2026, as of September 24, 2026– Economic Research– BNP Paribas
Advanced economies: Resilience to shock after shock

Document 232: Eurozone _ Growth on track despite the turbulence (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone _ Growth on track despite the turbulence (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

8
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
GROWTH ON TRACK DESPITE THE TURBULENCE
Growth was stronger than expected in Q2 and is set to strengthen further in the coming quarters. According to our fore-
casts, it will reach 1.5% in 2027 after 1.1% in 2026. This growth is expected to be driven by investment and its knock-on
effect on exports. On an annual average basis, inflation is expected to stand at 3% in 2026 and 2.8% in 2027, peaking at

the end of 2026. The ECB is expected to continue its monetary tightening. At this stage, a single further rate rise, aimed at
keeping inflation expectations anchored, is expected in December. The impact of rising interest rates (key policy rates and
market rates) on public finances and private sector borrowing conditions will be worth monitoring.
GROWTH: BEYOND THE SHOCKS, A RESILIENT MOMENTUM
Growth in the Eurozone remained robust in the first half of 2026, at

0.25% q/q in Q1 and 0.33% q/q in Q2, excluding Ireland (where economic
activity is volatile). It was driven by Germany, where signs of a recovery
are becoming clearer (see Germany). Our baseline scenario forecasts
growth of 1.1% in 2026, followed by 1.5% in 2027 on an annual average
basis. This growth is expected to be driven by the strengthening of
trends already underway in public and private investment (particularly
in defence and AI) and in exports (trade in services and intra-zone trade

in goods). Household consumption is likely to be resilient too. According
to our forecasts, job creation would continue to underpin household
incomes, thereby cushioning the impact of inflation.
A NEW LEASE OF LIFE FOR INDUSTRY
The Eurozone is undergoing one of the most profound industrial trans-
formations in its history. This is giving rise to divergences between key
sectors but, above all, to new drivers of growth whose effects are beco-
ming increasingly apparent (see our analysis). Indeed, the signs of an

economic recovery are growing stronger. Business sentiment indicators
show a marked improvement in the economic outlook across most sec-
tors (Chart 2), driven primarily by electronics (AI, digitalisation), the
automotive sector and aerospace. European industry is thus benefi -
ting from strong demand for capital goods; a structural trend that will
continue to be fuelled by ongoing investment cycles in AI, defence and
electrification. The introduction of minimum European content quotas

in the draft law on the industrial accelerator, together with the Com -
mission’s intention to tighten the criteria for access to European public
procurement markets, will provide additional support for European in-
dustry in the longer term.
FOREIGN TRADE: A REPOSITIONING THAT IS PAYING OFF
Following a decline in 2026, linked to rising energy import costs, the
trade surplus is expected to rise to around 3.0% of GDP in 2027 (of
which 2% of GDP for goods and 1% for services). Growth in European ex-

ports has slowed compared with its pre-Covid trend and is being ham-
pered by the decline in Chinese and US markets. However, Eurozone
exports in volume rose significantly in Q2 (+3.4% q/q).
More importantly, since Liberation Day (April 2025), Eurozone coun -
tries have found significant sources of growth amongst other European
partners, which has enabled them to cushion the decline in exports to
certain third markets. This is evident in exports to the rest of the EU,

EFTA countries (Switzerland in particular) and, to a lesser extent, the
United Kingdom, Ukraine and the Balkans (Chart 3). The momentum of
intra-European trade therefore remains very strong. It is expected to
strengthen further with the anticipated investments in defence, AI and
electrification.
FINANCING: FINANCIAL CONDITIONS ARE EXPECTED TO REMAIN TIGHT
Financial conditions in the Eurozone remained tight in Q2 2026, accor-
ding to indices calculated by the ECB (including the BIG [Broad Inter -

mediation Gauge]). They are expected to tighten more significantly in
the second half of the year: i/ the rise in long-term bond yields, which
has accelerated since the outbreak of the conflict in Iran, is expected to
continue; and ii/ the ECB will continue its monetary tightening (+25 bps

### Page 2

9
EcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
in Q3, then in Q4). For the time being, the debt burden is still weighing
only moderately on public finances due to the long average maturity of
countries’ debt (8.2 years on average in the Eurozone in July), but this
burden will increase by 2030.
On the corporate front, the rise in interest rates has remained contained
(+2 bps since January 2026, reaching 3.9% in July), which has contri -

buted to the strong growth in new investment loans (cumulated over
one year, +9.1% y/y in July compared with 15.3% y/y in September 2025).
By contrast, cumulative net debt securities issues over one year re -
mained stable in July, whilst cumulative net issues of listed shares over
one year halved month on month.
Rising interest rates will continue to hamper new variable-rate
home loans to households (around 65% of new lending) more signi -
ficantly than fixed-rate loans, for which rates stood in July at 3.69%

(+4 bps m/m) and 3.36% (+2 bps m/m) respectively. New home loans,
which are stable in 2026 on a cumulative over one year basis, are there-
fore unlikely to see any improvement. A fall in real-estate transactions
could weigh on new consumer credit through a reduction in expenditure
on home furnishings and renovations, whereas consumer credit has
been fairly buoyant so far (year-to-date, +5.2% y/y in July compared
with +1.4% y/y in January).
LABOUR MARKET: RESILIENCE YET TO BE CONFIRMED

Employment in the Eurozone rose again by an average of 0.1% q/q in
the first half of 2026, driven by Spain and Portugal. Given the growth
outlook in these two countries, this momentum is likely to continue,
whilst business sentiment surveys in Germany suggest that employ -
ment is beginning to stabilise. Nevertheless, employment levels are
plateauing (France, the Netherlands) or even falling in other countries
(Austria, Finland), which is likely to slow the decline in the Eurozone

unemployment rate, meaning it is expected to remain close to current
levels in 2027. Furthermore, second-round effects of the rise in inflation
on wages cannot be ruled out: the ECB’s wage tracker suggests wage
growth of close to 3% by summer 2027 (2.9% y/y in June 2027, compared
with 2.6% in September).
INFLATION: A PEAK EXPECTED IN LATE 2026
Changes in wage dynamics will be a key factor in the inflation out -
look for 2027, whilst energy price rises are picking up again, driven

by the conflict in the Middle East. This rise is even sharper than in
the spring for gas (linked to restocking ahead of winter) and is likely
to feed through to electricity prices in several countries. We therefore
anticipate inflation peaking at 3.7% y/y in Q4 2026, before easing as the
energy component falls. On an annual average, headline inflation is
expected to stand at 3% in 2026 and then at 2.8% in 2027.
At this stage, the pass-through of rising energy prices to other com -

ponents of the consumer price index, particularly services, remains li -
mited. The alternative core indicators monitored by the ECB, including
the PCCI (Persistent and Common Component of Inflation, 2.1% in July),
remain close to the 2% target. Core inflation, however, is expected to
peak two months later than headline inflation (3% in March 2027).
Inflationary risks are nevertheless tilted to the upside. These are lin -
ked to food prices, which are expected to be affected by the energy

shock and weather conditions (summer drought, El Niño); they could
also stem from a delayed impact of energy price rises on underlying
inflation. The rise in prices of energy-dependent inputs (notably che -
micals and plastics) and those linked to the AI sector is expected to be
sustained. Growth, which we expect to remain robust over the coming
quarters, will also fuel underlying inflationary pressures.
MONETARY POLICY: TIGHTENING WITHOUT SLOWING (GROWTH)

Following two 25-basis-point rises (in June and September), we ex -
pect a third in December, which would bring the deposit facility rate
to 2.75%. It would then be in slightly restrictive territory (we estimate
the upper bound of the neutral rate to be 2.5%). This rise would be
aimed primarily at anchoring inflation expectations and, consequently,
at curbing the pass-through effects of inflation. Monetary tightening
would therefore remain limited. Indeed, the overshoot of the inflation

target would be temporary, and the operational framework adopted by
the ECB in June 2025 allows it to tolerate this, provided it remains brief.
However, it cannot be ruled out that the ECB might take further action
if the decline in inflation proves slower than expected.
PUBLIC FINANCES ARE CONTINGENT ON RISING INTEREST RATES
The fiscal impulse in the Eurozone is expected to be clearly positive in
2026 due to the rise in defence and infrastructure spending in Germany.

This will contribute to a widening of the country’s primary deficit (from
+0.9 pp of GDP to 2.8%, according to our forecasts). The improvement in
public finances is expected to stall in both France and Italy, at least in
2026, due to the impact of inflation on the debt burden via index-linked
bonds. This trend is expected to reverse in 2027 with less expansionary
fiscal policies. Nevertheless, the increase in the debt service burden
would fuel a further rise in the public deficit, which would thus reach

around 3.3% and 3.5% of GDP in 2026 and 2027 respectively (compared
with 2.9% in 2025). Against this backdrop, the Eurozone’s public debt-
to-GDP ratio is projected to exceed 90% of GDP in 2026 and reach 93%
of GDP in 2027, according to our forecasts.
Article completed on 15 September 2026

Document 242: Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us

Source PDF: all report pdf/Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

From better ﬁscal headroom to surprise income tax hikes, this
is how UK Chancellor John Healey's ﬁrst budget could surprise
investors
James Smith
UK Chancellor John Healey
In a nutshell: What we expect
Higher debt interest costs look set to drive the level of 'ﬁscal
headroom' from £24bn in March to £10bn. There's a growing
sense that Chancellor John Healey would like to get this up
to £15bn or higher. The government is set to modestly
increase spending on the cost of living, though the total

intervention is unlikely to meaningfully top £5bn and it's
likely to be mostly billed as temporary to avoid counting
against the main ﬁscal rule. We expect an increase in capital
spending of roughly £10bn/year, bearing in mind this is
exempt from the ﬁscal rule dictating that the current budget
must be in surplus after ﬁve years. To rebuild headroom

### Page 2

against that rule, we expect £5-6bn of tax hikes, likely via a
higher Bank Surcharge and possibly broadening the National
Insurance tax base.
In short, we don't expect the budget to deliver a material
stimulus (or add materially to the already-planned ﬁscal
consolidation). The impact on the Bank of England, which is
poised to hike rates in November, is likely to be fairly
minimal.
But all chancellors like to keep a rabbit in their hat – and what
follows are ﬁve ways it could surprise us.

1. Fiscal headroom goes up – not down
What if the UK’s ﬁscal hole isn’t nearly as bad as we think?
The widespread expectation going into the UK’s Autumn Budget on
28 October is that the Chancellor is going to have to face up to
higher debt interest costs with some modest tax hikes.
Bank of England rate expectations are almost a percentage point
higher than they were at the time of the Oﬃce for Budget
Responsibility’s March assessment of Britain’s public ﬁnances. 10-

year bond yields are some 70 basis points higher, which, taken
together, will push up debt interest spending by £11bn/year by
2029/30.
That points to a sizeable reduction in the amount of money the
government has left under its main ﬁscal rule, which requires the
current budget (day-to-day spending, including debt interest less
revenue) to be in surplus within three years. Back in March, that
surplus, or ﬁscal headroom, was projected at £24bn in 2029/30.

Debt interest costs are rising
Cookie settings

### Page 3

*Based on the average rate in the 10 days up to 25 September, which
likely represents the OBR's observation window for this budget
Source: Macrobond, ING
We think that headroom will have dropped to around £10bn before
any corrective action. And the vibe from the Treasury suggests the
Chancellor would prefer it to be above £15bn, to give more
breathing space in case of future adverse forecast revisions. That
would point to some limited tax hikes.
But what if that’s all wrong? The truth is, nobody really knows how

the OBR will change its assessment of the UK economy. And as we
saw last year, the Treasury is often inclined to talk up the doom and
gloom to open up a positive surprise on the day. 12 months ago,
the hit to headroom was considerably smaller than had been
widely expected.
This time, any positive surprise is most likely to come from wage
growth. Will the rise in energy prices spill into higher pay, just as it
did in 2022? We think it won’t, not least because the jobs market is

much weaker, an assessment we suspect the OBR will share.
But even relatively small upgrades to wage growth can make a big
diﬀerence to the government’s fortunes. Since 2010, the OBR has
revised the level of earnings across its ﬁve-year forecast by an
average of 1.3 percentage points in absolute terms. If it did that
now, say by lifting wage growth over the next two years by 0.6ppt
apiece, it would add £10bn to headroom, almost perfectly
oﬀsetting the hit from higher interest costs.

### Page 4

There are also reasons to think the impact of lower migration
projections, which some reports suggest could wipe another £7bn
oﬀ headroom via lower tax revenue forecasts, could be much less
than feared. The starting point for employment looks a little higher
than it did back in March. The rise in global equities this year should
also be helpful for capital gains tax projections.
Why does this all matter? Because the higher the headroom, the
less ‘need’ there is to hike taxes, and if it is signiﬁcantly higher than

expected, the more room there is to increase spending under the
ﬁscal rules.
The simple message is this: don’t judge the Autumn Budget by the
level of headroom left over.
2. Spending splurge
The problem with ‘headroom’ is not only that it’s highly sensitive to
the OBR’s economic outlook, but that the forward-looking nature of
the ﬁscal rules opens the door to a lot of creative accounting.
The impact of higher inﬂation is a case in point. This helps the

Treasury through higher receipts – both VAT and income tax. Yes, it
also pushes up the cost of indexed beneﬁts. But importantly,
departmental budgets don’t automatically increase to oﬀset the
impact of higher prices.
That opens up a classic trick from the Treasury, which is to leave
spending plans virtually unchanged in nominal terms, setting up
substantially smaller real-terms budget increases across the
medium term.
Even before the Iran War, government plans pencilled in real-terms

cuts to per capita departmental spending in FY2029. Given some
functions, like health, are ‘protected’, that implies much deeper cuts
to smaller departments.
Suﬃce it to say, that is unrealistic. Higher inﬂation will push up the
wage bill across government. It would cost £12bn to simply
maintain those March departmental spending plans in real terms.
That’s before considering the cost of ending that austerity pencilled

please conﬁrm your preferences. You can change your cookie

Targeting Cookies

### Page 5

in for later this decade, or how the government will fund its defence
or social care ambitions.
A top-up to government departments is inevitable – the question is
when. It looks like the Treasury wants to kick the can on these tricky
budget decisions until next autumn, when it plans to release its
next Spending Review, a process of allocating money across
departments. No doubt the hope is that debt interest costs will
have fallen and the economic outlook brightened, such that there's

more space under the ﬁscal rules to make bigger spending
commitments.
That points to a very modest package of spending measures this
month, mainly targeting the cost of living. On top of the already-
announced cut to VAT on energy, we’d imagine the Treasury will
replicate last year’s budget and lower household bills by funding a
greater share of ‘policy costs’ from general taxation. This has the
added beneﬁt of lowering headline inﬂation at a particularly

sensitive moment for the Bank of England. Crucially though, the
government is likely to bill these moves as temporary, so they don’t
count under that main ﬁscal rule.
But if the economic picture really is better than feared, don’t rule
out the government going further. A decision to fully fund the UK’s
3% defence spending commitment would be a major wildcard,
though this would likely come at the expense of capital plans in
other areas of government.

Ultimately, the key thing to watch is the OBR’s assessment of how
all these policy changes aﬀect GDP growth and borrowing over the
next year. If there’s a material stimulus eﬀect, it could add to
market bets on UK rate hikes.
3. A manifesto break on income tax
Tax hikes are likely at the Autumn Budget, but should be very small.
We’re probably looking at a package of circa £5bn (0.2% of GDP).
Part of the problem is that the list of viable options looks perilously

small if Labour is to avoid breaking its manifesto pledge not to raise

Is there scope for a surprise? We think there are two main ways on
tax – one good, one bad.
The good? A surprise announcement of a so-called ‘tax switch’, a
novel proposal from Resolution Foundation which would see the
government simultaneously raise basic income tax and cut

employee National Insurance by 2ppt. The impact on workers
would be neutral, but because income tax has a broader base
(including pensions), this would raise £6bn/year.
Though not a substantial sum, it would signal to investors that
Prime Minister Andy Burnham isn’t afraid to go near the thorny
issue of raising taxes. But in light of recent pension changes,

including to the infamous ‘triple lock’, this feels like too much of a

The negative surprise would be if the Treasury decided to raise the

would also signal that the UK’s commitment to the threshold freeze
– which is doing the lion's share of the planned ﬁscal consolidation
– is waning.
A big rise in the National Living Wage

The minimum wage increase in April 2027, which will be
announced alongside the budget, is perhaps the most important
variable for the Bank of England.
This is based on a recommendation from the Low Pay Commission,
which earlier this year provisionally estimated the increase at 3.7%.
That’s a smaller increase than over the past two years and,
crucially, is not a million miles above the level the Bank considers
consistent with a 2% medium-term inﬂation target (3.25%).

But the LPC has some ﬂexibility in its recommendation, and one
criterion is the cost of living. Higher inﬂation means we could see a
ﬁnal proposal between 4% and 4.5%. Anything above that would,
we suspect, be problematic for the Bank of England and would
bolster the case for higher rates.
The government is no doubt well aware of this. And there appears
to be growing recognition among Labour ﬁgures, including on the
left of the party, that the big minimum wage increases, coupled

with previous payroll tax increases, have had a big impact on
business – particularly in consumer services.
But if the LPC were to recommend minimum wage growth of close
to 5%, could the government resist it? In theory, yes. But politically,
we suspect not.
A surge in bond issuance
We already know that bond issuance is set to increase again in
2027, though for reasons unrelated to the UK’s ﬁscal trajectory.

Document 257: How bad is the rise in bond yields_ (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/How bad is the rise in bond yields_ (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-23
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

1

SPECIAL EDITION | HOW BAS IS THE RISE 
IN BOND YIELDS?

TRANSCRIPTION

0:00:10 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Everyone, welcome. As we head into autumn 2026, interest rates are reaching levels never seen for many, many years. 
Why is this happening? Is it sustainable? And what are the implications for the economies of different countries? All these 
questions will be discussed over the next few minutes with the economists of BNP Paribas. Let us begin with Isabelle 
Mateos y Lago, chief economist of the group. Hello, Isabelle.  
0:00:38 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Hello Emmanuel, hello everyone. 
0:00:39 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you for being with us today. Just to begin, a simple explanation. What rates are we talking about and which countries 
are affected? 
0:00:48 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Yes, it is very important to clarify this. So, we are talking about long-term interest rates, rates at 10 years or longer 
maturities, 30 years, etc. The rates at which sovereign states borrow on the market. And this is primarily something that is 
happening in advanced countries, so United States, United King dom, Japan, and then the Eurozone countries. And indeed, 
as you said, we are seeing since the beginning of the summer, interest rates reach levels we had not seen for about 20 
years. But it is important to keep in mind that up until that point, up until the mid -2000s, the level of interest rates that 
we're seeing now were really quite common, quite normal, so one shouldn't overdramatize.  
0:01:35 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Why do we find ourselves in this situation and how did we get to this point?  
0:01:41 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Yes, so the direct cause is that investors are demanding higher interest rates in order to  lend their capital to governments. 
But why is this? Well, there's essentially three reasons for this, and not all of them are bad, and they're not mutually 
exclusive either. The top reason to my mind is that there's just greater competition for capital be tween sovereign states 
and the corporate sector, who are all facing simultaneously additional investment needs, in defense, in artificial 
intelligence, in energy transition, in resilience. And so,  you just have greater competition for capital and that pushes up the 
price of capital, which is the interest rate. 
Reason number two is because markets have revised upwards their expectations of how high central banks are going to be 
taking their interest rates. 
And this mechanically translates into higher rates at the long end. And then there's a third reason, which doesn't apply 
necessarily in every case, but which is essentially higher sovereign risk, a kind of a risk premium, in cases where there are  
concerns arising about the sustainability of these public debts.

### Page 2

2

0:02:59 - Emmanuel Laborde 
What are the implications for global economies? 
0:03:03 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
So that's exactly what we'll be discussing in the next segments. But basically, the two key issues are, one, an adverse 
impact on public finances. It's higher interest rates making it harder to balance the books in terms of public accounts. And 
then secondly, these higher rates tend to slow down economic growth. Of course, by how much and how serious it will be, 
depends on how high interest rates go and how long they stay there. But that's for the next segments.  
0:03:38 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Yes. Thank you very much, Isabelle. Having said the scene now, let's move on to the first segment with the following 
question. What makes debt sustainable? And let's see what Hélène Baudchon has to say.  
Hello Hélène and welcome into this special edition. 
0:03:58 - Hélène Baudchon 
Hello Emmanuel, hello everybody. 
0:04:01 - Emmanuel Laborde 
When economists talk about public debt, they often refer to the sustainability of debt. As an economist, can you explain to 
us what that means and what is the debt trajectory deemed unsustainable?  
0:04:15 - Hélène Baudchon 
To begin with, I will say that a temporary increase in debt can be seen as a normal evolution, not particularly concerning, 
for instance during an economic crisis or more recently during a health crisis. In such cases, it acts as a buffer. A problem  
arises when the debt level never comes back down. An unsustainable trajectory can be therefore defined, characterized by 
a continuous regular rise in the debt level, both in absolute terms and as a proportion of GDP, driven by high and recurring 
fiscal deficits. And sometimes interest payments can exacerbate the problem.  
0:05:00 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In very simple terms, you are talking about the snowball effect.  
0:05:03 - Hélène Baudchon 
Exactly explaining why consolidation measures are needed in order to prevent such an adverse situation from occurring 
and in order to prevent, in fact, a sudden loss of confidence from creditors.  
0:05:16 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Regarding governments, what should serve as a warning sign for them? And at what point could the situation become, let's 
say, problematic? 
0:05:27 - Hélène Baudchon 
Well, simply speaking, when interest rates rise, not so long ago, up until 2022, Eurozone countries were borrowing at very 
low rates and in some instance, even negative rates. And although debt levels were already at high level at that time, the 
low rates helped manage debt servicing. Not much fiscal effort was needed to keep the public debt ratios in check.  
0:05:55 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Of course, we all remember this because it was a very unusual situation, but now things are very different.

### Page 3

3

0:06:01 - Hélène Baudchon 
Yes, indeed, the situation today is clearly more challenging simply because bond yields are significantly higher and they 
are rising at the time of our discussion. This is for the part the result of the ECB's monetary tightening in 2022 -2023 aimed 
at addressing the inflation shock then. Then long-term interest rates have risen as a consequence and have aligned more 
closely with GDP growth rate. And in technical terms, this is what we refer to as a narrowing of the "r" - "g" spread. 
0:06:41 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Why is this spread so important? Why do you, the economists, monitor it this way?  
0:06:48 - Hélène Baudchon 
Essentially, you have the interest rate "r", which determines the debt burden, while the growth rate "g" determines the 
trajectory of fiscal revenues. And if "r" is lower than "g", and this has been the case for many years, as you can see on the  
chart on the screen, it means that interest payments are increasing at a slower pace than the revenue component of the 
equation. 
0:07:14 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And this provides some leeway in other budgetary areas, such as education, of course, defense, maybe pensions.  
0:07:22 - Hélène Baudchon 
Yes, indeed. And this is what we refer to as the primary budget. However, if and when interest rates do go up while the 
economy isn't growing that much faster, then fiscal leeway diminishes.  
0:07:35 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Crystal clear. Now, just could you provide a few last words to wrap up the sustainability segment?  
0:07:43 - Hélène Baudchon 
Well, to conclude, we are entering a period which demands greater fiscal discipline or, as some might say, less indiscipline 
in order to manage debt trajectories effectively, in particular in the countries which have high debt levels. And for instanc e, 
the Italian case is an interesting one because it is well known for its high debt level ratio. But in fact, it also has an 
advantageous position because it has no primary deficit. In fact, it even benefits from a large primary surplus.  
0:08:22 - Emmanuel Laborde 
As for the countries... For the other countries... I stop you because this will be a discussion with Guillaume Derrien during  
the next segment. Thank you very much, Hélène. 
0:08:30 - Hélène Baudchon 
Thank you, Emmanuel. 
0:08:36 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Hello, Guillaume. Welcome. 
0:08:37 - Guillaume Derrien 
Thank you. 
0:08:38 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Hello, Emmanuel, and hello to everyone. The flexibility of fiscal policy depends heavily on interest rates, which have 
increased considerably over the past few months. The question is, where do we stand today?

### Page 4

4

0:08:52 - Guillaume Derrien 
So, long-term rates have increased in all advanced economies, reaching levels not seen since 2008 in Europe and in the 
United States, and even since 1996 in Japan. Fortunately, the effective rate that states pay on their debt is lower than thos e 
headline figures suggest, simply because the average maturity of debt is relatively long. Precisely how long are we talking 
about? So, it's about 10 years in Japan, 13 years in the United Kingdom, and 8.5 years in France. That means that most of 
the debt was raised before rates started climbing. 
For example, in France, the effective rate just recently rose above the 2% mark, which is significantly lower than the 10 -
year bond yield, which exceeded 4% for new issuances.  
That said, in the United States, which has an average maturity of roughly six years, is experiencing a more rapid increase in  
its effective rate. 
0:09:54 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And this rate increase is happening in the context of high debt -to-GDP ratios and with modest growth, correct? Yes, that's 
correct. 
0:10:05 - Guillaume Derrien 
Debt levels have reached unprecedented heights since the end of World War II. And at the same time, growth is lower, 
hovering around 2% on average in the United States, 1% in Europe and 0.5% in Japan. What does that mean for debt 
servicing costs? So, debt servicing cost is already relatively high, but it is expected to grow further as debt issue before the 
2022 rate hikes mature and are replaced with new instruments bearing higher interest rates. By 2030, interest costs could 
rise to levels last seen in 2000, specifically in France, around 4% of GDP. 
Are all the countries equally affected by these trends? Actually, no. Some countries are managing to reduce their debt 
level. 
Spain, Italy, even Japan have sufficiently improved their primary balance to achieve this, at least for the time being. And o f 
course, we must speak about Germany. Yes, Germany is implementing investment programs that result in a higher public 
deficit and debt-to-GDP ratio. But this ratio started from a relatively low base at around above 60% of GDP. That said, in 
this current context, German bonds have risen. And because German bonds are used as a benchmark for Europe, it  raises 
rates across all Eurozone countries. Is France the only country seeing its public debt go up?  
No, it's not, Emmanuel. This is happening in the United Kingdom and in the United States. These economies, like France, 
are running public deficits that are ever close or exceed 5% of GDP, which is too large for debt ratio to stabilize on its ow n. 
France and the United Kingdom are trying to gradually reduce their public deficit so as not to compromise growth. The 
United States is not going on that path yet, but market pressure could eventually force them to do so.  
0:12:08 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much for your input, Guillaume. Thank you, Emmanuel. Let's go to the next segment. It's about the impact 
of rising bond yields on financing and investments in the Eurozone.  
0:12:26 - Thomas Humblot 
Hello, Thomas. Welcome. Hello. Thank you for having me today.  
0:12:30 - Emmanuel Laborde 
We've just seen what's behind the rise in Eurozone bond yields. This rise is partly attributed to the sovereign spread. What 
impact will this have on the economy? 
0:12:42 - Thomas Humblot 
Actually, this widening of some government bond spreads is obviously not good news, but it tends paradoxically to have 
less impact than an increase in long-term interest rates that does not include the sovereign risk premium. for example, in 
France, for a very long time, it has been considered that the 10 -year OAT was the relevant benchmark for fixed loans. And 
at that time, the spread on French government bonds was relatively stable. But since 2024, that spread increased

### Page 5

5

significantly and that renders the sovereign yield a less relevant benchmark.  
And now the most relevant benchmark is the swap rate because it determines the cost of bank funding that backs new 
loans. 
0:13:56 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Since the end of 2024, we have seen a rise in long-term 10-year swap rates. What impact does this have on bank lending 
rates? 
0:14:08 - Thomas Humblot 
Fixed loans have obviously become more expensive, but to a lesser extent than if they were priced solely based on long -
term sovereign rates. And what about existing fixed-rate loans? 
There is no impact. An increase in rates for the entire maturity of a loan does not have any effect if it is a fixed -rate loan. 
Borrowers with outstanding loans are therefore protected. Obviously, new fixed loans are granted at higher rates. And this 
sharply contrasts with a floating-rates loans. 
Because with floating-rates loans, borrowers experience regular rate adjustments throughout the term of the loan. And in 
terms of bank financing? Strictly in terms of bank financing, an increase in short -term rates has an immediate and 
widespread restrictive impact on borrowers, while for long-term interest rates, the impact is more gradual, and it comes 
gradually as the outstanding amount of fixed-rate loans are progressively renewed. And the thing is that since the 
beginning of the war in Iran, we have seen a combination of the two.  
And in practical terms, the rate of new bank loans to non-financial corporations has increased about 25 basis points in the 
eurozone since the beginning of the year. 
0:16:12 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Just a few words about companies that borrow directly from the market.  
0:16:16 - Thomas Humblot 
Well, in that case, the impact is even more straightforward. The rate at which a company borrows from the market is the 
risk-free rate plus a premium. 
0:16:28 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Have risk premiums, known as corporate credit spreads, also increased?  
0:16:34 - Thomas Humblot 
Yes, they have increased temporarily at the beginning of the year but have returned to normal since. For the time being, the 
widening remains moderate for the highest-rated investment-grade issuers. 
However, some significant bond issuance in the euro debt market due to some major US technology companies has 
increased spreads for longer maturities. 
0:17:11 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In particular terms, how does this affect the investment decisions of the companies?  
0:17:19 - Thomas Humblot 
Well, investments that were previously worthwhile due to returns sufficient to cover the cost of capital may no longer do so 
and in turn that might lead to a decrease in demand for financing.  
0:17:40 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much Thomas. Now let us move on to the emerging economies. And it is with Cynthia Kalasopatan -Antoine.

### Page 6

6

Hello Cynthia, and welcome. 
0:17:54 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Hi, Emmanuel. Hello, everyone. 
0:17:57 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Long-term bond yields rose significantly in advanced economies over the summer. What can we now say about emerging 
markets? 
0:18:06 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Emerging bond markets held up well, which is quite unusual. The rise in long -term sovereign yields in emerging countries 
has been less pronounced than in advanced economies, including the United States and Germany. Now, there are two 
main exceptions, Turkey and Poland. Meanwhile, we did not witness any significant correction in emerging financial 
markets over the summer, and the main currencies appreciated slightly against the dollar or the euro.  
0:18:37 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Does this mean that public finances are more robust in emerging countries?  
0:18:43 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, it depends. You see, in cases like the Czech Republic and South Korea, public debt is moderate and macroeconomic 
fundamentals are solid. However, these countries are now less frequently considered as emerging and tend to be viewed 
as newly advanced economies. By contrast, Brazil and South Africa have weaker public finances and investors have 
already incorporated a higher sovereign risk into their assessments. And this is reflected in the bond yields. And this 
assessment of sovereign risk did not really change over the summer. 
0:19:26 - Emmanuel Laborde 
When comparing the main fiscal metrics, what are the trends? What do you see as an economist?  
0:19:33 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
On average, the fiscal metrics in emerging markets are not substantially worse than those in advanced economies. The 
average budget deficit levels are similar, ranging from 4% to 5% of GDP in 2025 -2026, according to the IMF. And as in 
advanced economies, the public debt is higher than its pre-Covid level. However, the average public debt to GDP ratio in 
emerging countries excluding China is close to 60%, whereas in advanced economies, it is above 100%.  
0:20:12 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Do you arrive at the same conclusion when comparing debt sustainability criteria?  
0:20:17 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, there are significant regional differences. In emerging Asia, the factors that determine debt sustainability are more 
often favorable than those in advanced economies. In Central Europe, the gap between the interest rate paid on debt and 
the GDP growth rate is also favorable. However, the primary deficits in some countries are high.  
0:20:45 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In a few words, a brief overview of the situation in Latin America.  
0:20:49 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, Brazil and Mexico are in a less favorable position. These countries would indeed need to generate a significant 
primary budget surplus each year in order to stabilize their debt in the medium term.

### Page 7

7

0:21:07 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Economists use the term original sin, which refers to the situation when governments resort to borrowing in foreign 
currency to address the shortfall in domestic financing. Does this phenomenon continue to distinguish emerging 
countries? 
0:21:24 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Yes, this is still the case. Emerging countries still have a significant share of their debt in foreign currency. This repres ents 
about 35 to 40% of their total debt on average. And in fact, now, this figure has decreased over the past decade, and this 
contributes to their resilience. However, governments in emerging markets have a less favorable position as regards their 
debt profile and their financing costs are also on average higher.  
0:22:02 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And finally, just a few words about a larger subject, China.  
0:22:07 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
China's public debt is significant and growing rapidly. That being said, it is denominated in domestic currency and issued in  
domestic markets. This debt is, however, sustainable in the medium term due to a very low effective interest rate and a 
high growth rate. 
0:22:29 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much, Cynthia, for being with us today.  
0:22:32 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Thank you. 
0:22:33 - Emmanuel Laborde 
It is now time to conclude, and we welcome back Group Chief Economist Isabelle Mateos y Lago.  
It is time for our conclusion with Isabelle. Once again, just a final question, please. Could or should, I do not know which 
word is the good one, central banks intervene to curb this rise in interest rates?  
0:22:58 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Absolutely not. The main contribution that central banks can bring to dealing with this situation is to restore price stabili ty, 
as is their mandate, as promptly as possible. And in the current circumstances, this means keeping relatively high policy 
rates, in some cases, rising them further. And the responsibility for keeping public finances on a sustainable footing rests 
squarely with governments and any attempt by these governments to instead take the easy way out by pressuring central 
banks to lower interest rates prematurely would be enormously counterproductive.  
0:23:41 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much, Isabelle. Thank you all for joining us today. Just a few words to remind you that additional economic 
research content can be found by scanning the QR code shown on your screen. Do not hesitate to use it with your 
smartphone. It is very easy. It was a pleasure to be with you today. Have a nice day and hope to see you so

► 隨人工智慧運算需求持續攀升 ，谷歌、亞馬
遜、臉書、微軟及OpenAI等企業巨頭積極發
展客製化晶片 (ASIC)，藉此提升運算效率並
優化成 本 。根據 凱 基投顧 引述產 業數 據預
估，2027年客製化晶片出貨量將年增 92%至
1,446萬顆 ，首度超 越繪圖處理器 (GPU)的
1,129萬顆 ，顯示AI晶片市場正由 GPU主導
逐步轉向多元化發展。
► 在客製化晶片方面 ，谷歌持續擴大自研張量
處理器 (TPU)的應用與對外供應 ，亞馬遜亦
積極拓展自研AI晶片(Trainium)的雲端運算服
務，預估兩者合計將佔 2027年客製化晶片出
貨量約80%。
► 隨客製 化晶 片需 求 快速增 加 ，相 關晶 片設
計、先 進製 程及 先 進封裝 需求可 望同 步提
升，涉及上述業務發展的台股供應鏈 (註)有機
會受到市場關注。

### Page 6

6
投資焦點
► 2024年全球工廠運作的工業機器人約 470萬
台，為10年前的 3倍，主要為韓國 、中國和
日本等製造業大國將其運用於汽車 、電子和
金屬加工等產業 ，並因而維持成本和品質優
勢，為競爭力重要來源之一 。雖然美國工業
機器人的普及程度仍低於亞洲主要製造業國
家，但近年積極尋求重建工業產能 、將關鍵
供應鏈遷回美國國內以及擴大半導體和製藥
生產，有可能讓美國逐步調整工業和製造層
面的策略方向。
► 傳統工業機器人與人形機器人在外觀設計與
應用場景上有明顯差異 。工業機器人通常部
署於高度標準化的環境 ，以執行範圍相對有
限且重複性高的特定作業為主 ，而人形機器
人則被設計用於多變的環境 ，並經常需要與
人類協同工作 ，因此需結合移動能力 、感知
能力與靈巧操作能力 ，才能夠執行多樣化任
務，而非受限單一功能。
工業機器人技術相對成熟且部署速度較快全球運作中的工業機器人數量穩定成長
工業機器人技術成熟且穩定成長，為製造業強國的重要競爭力來源

### Page 7

7
投資焦點人形機器人
► 人 形機 器 人 主 要由 結 合半 導 體 與AI 模 型的
「大腦」，並由感測器 、執行器 、線材、連
接器及輕量化電池驅動的機身所組成 ，可透
過感知環境資訊 、思考並進行協調的實體動
作。投行預估至2030年，執行器將佔整體人
形機器人成本超過 50%，為價值含量最高的
核心零組件 ，麥肯錫公司甚至認為執行器與
感測器可能成為未來供應鏈中的關鍵瓶頸。
► 機器人在實體世界中執行任務時 ，任何延遲
或連線中斷都可能帶來重大風險 ，因此相較
純數位的 AI應用 ，對即時運算 (邊緣運算 )能
力的要求更高 。而近幾年邊緣運算軟硬體能
力大幅提升 ，使本地端設備得以部署數十億
參數的 AI模型 ，並將反應延遲縮短至約 0.3
秒，使機器人能夠即時感知 、決策與行動 。
此外，過去6年風險資本對機器人、感測器及
IT 硬體的每年平均投資約 100 億美元 ， 但
2026年僅上半年即已達 280億美元 ，暗示市
場對機器人產業潛力的期待有別於過往。
機器人技術進展快速並開始吸引風險資本投資人形機器人運作有賴AI模型與大量機身零組件整合

人形機器人產業結合AI與硬體，含金量高吸引風險資本進駐

### Page 8

8
股市
◆ 美、歐、日央行升息抑通膨 ，公債殖利率走高壓抑股債市場近期動能 。然而包括油價自高檔回落使
通膨壓力稍緩 ，以及美股第三季獲利年增率仍為雙位數成長 ，股市表現有支撐 。市場持續觀察AI資
本支出是否帶來獲利成長 ，短期內評價面壓力將增添市場動盪 ，可分散配置於不同國家與產業 ，降
低市場波動影響 。總結上述，地緣衝突、殖利率偏高及評價面壓力等紛擾帶來負面影響 ，可布局防
禦股抗震，長期而言，科技及AI半導體發展趨勢不變且基本面佳，可逢拉回時分批布局。
◆ 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，加上全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍
工仍有投資題材。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
◆ 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，且最新聯準會利率點陣圖顯示 ，官員預估今年底前仍有一碼升息空間 ，立場
偏向維持較高利率水平抑制通膨發展 ，加上9月製造業及服務業PMI優於預期，殖利率偏高環境建議

以中短天期債券投資為主 ，可逢利率走升時布局進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流
與還款能力，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
◆ 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
◆ 聯準會升息且態度偏鷹，美元高檔盤整。
◆ 日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，未如市場預期的鷹派，日圓震盪。
美元高檔整理，日圓震盪
商品
◆ 中東地緣衝突加劇推升油價，短期紛擾難解，偏高的油價可能對通膨及實體經濟帶來影響。
◆ 短期受美國雙率走高有壓 ，惟美日財政赤字壓力與去美元化使全球央行買盤依舊 ，提供黃金中長期
穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法

### Page 15

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

Document 332: Top of Mind_ Higher bond yields_ how concerning_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Top of Mind_ Higher bond yields_ how concerning_.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 31
Status: ok

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Global bond yields have risen to multi-decade highs, yet growth and markets have
remained remarkably resilient. But with some pressures beginning to emerge (e.g. in
European sovereign bond markets), can this resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities
be revealed? We ask the IMF’s Tobias Adrian, Moody’s Analytics’ Mark Zandi, and GS
economists. Adrian is relatively sanguine about the economy’s resilience, though
notes downside risks, while GS economists expect only a modest growth drag from

higher rates, but a more consequential one if yields remain near current or higher
levels. Zandi is generally more concerned, describing the US economy as vulnerable
rather than resilient, and sees one-in-three odds of the economy going off the rails.
Meanwhile, GS strategists and analysts are not especially concerned about banks or
markets more broadly, but warn that further increases in yields or rate volatility could
test risk assets. Key to watch: whether the increasingly interconnected and leveraged

AI investment boom can continue to withstand higher rates.
Tobias Adrian, Special Advisor and former Director of Monetary and Capital Markets, IMF
“The global economy has demonstrated considerable resilience in the face of multiple severe shocks, and I would not be
surprised if it continues to shrug off higher yields, provided they do not keep rising sharply in a disorderly fashion. At the same
time, I do worry about downside risks.”
Mark Zandi, Chief Economist, Moody’s Analytics

Macro news and views

US Japan 
Latest GS proprietary datapoints/major changes in views 
• We lowered our 2026 core PCE inflation forecast to 3.0%    
(Q4/Q4, from 3.2%) after larger-than-expected downward 
revisions to yoy core PCE growth, which partly led us to push 
back our forecast for the next Fed hike to Dec (from Oct). 
Datapoints/trends we’re focused on 
• US economic growth, which has remained resilient this 
year despite higher rates and higher oil prices, though the 
economy is bifurcated, with strength among upper-income 
consumers and AI-exposed sectors. 
• US midterm elections, where prediction markets imply 
relatively high odds of a Democratic sweep as voters 
remain focused on higher gasoline prices. 
Latest GS proprietary datapoints/major changes in views 
• No major changes in views. 
Datapoints/trends we’re focused on 
• BoJ policy; we continue to expect the next rate hike in 
January 2027, though the timing could be brought forward 
in response to heightened inflation risks.  
• FY2027 Ministry of Finance budget request, which 
increased significantly from FY2026 to a record ¥143tn and 
includes larger-than-usual JGB issuance. 
• Japan real wages, which have maintained positive yoy 
growth since the beginning of the year.  
• Japan consumer sentiment, which declined in September.

Europe  Emerging Markets (EM) 
Latest GS proprietary datapoints/major changes in views 
• No major changes in views. 
Datapoints/trends we’re focused on 
• Positive growth spillovers from the global AI capex boom, 
which we think should continue into 2027.  
• UK growth, which we expect to slow in the next two quarters. 
• French elections; frontrunner Le Pen’s proposal to address 
fiscal challenges is likely to face implementation challenges. 
• Italy’s 2027/2028 fiscal deficit, which was revised up 
significantly, weakening the country’s debt outlook. 
• Rising sovereign stress, which has tightened EA financial 
conditions, reducing the urgency for the ECB to hike rates 
beyond Dec, when we expect the last hike. 
Latest GS proprietary datapoints/major changes in views 
• No major changes in views. 
Datapoints/trends we’re focused on  
• China’s export boom, which we find still has room to run 
over the next few years but will eventually require stronger 
economic growth in importing countries. 
• China FX policy; we see risk that RMB appreciation over-
shoots the gradual pace preferred by the PBOC, even as 
policymakers continue to lean against market expectations. 
• Asia’s AI dividend, which is broadening beyond exports. 
• Brazilian presidential elections, which will be settled in a 
second-round runoff on October 25, with a market-friendly 
election outcome likely to lead to further BRL appreciation.
